(45)

This spinor-valued 1-form can be split into its self-dual and anti-self-dual parts:

\omega_{AA^{\prime}BB^{\prime}}=A_{AB}\epsilon_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}+\tilde{A}_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\epsilon_{AB}.

(46)

The partial traces
A_{AB}=\frac{1}{2}\omega_{AA^{\prime}BB^{\prime}}\epsilon^{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
and
\tilde{A}_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}\omega_{AA^{\prime}BB^{\prime}}\epsilon^{AB}
are then called a self-dual and an anti-self-dual
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
connection respectively
3
3

3

Our naming convention matches those of e.g.
14
. An analogous relation holds between the curvature
F^{IJ}=\mathrm{d}\omega^{IJ}+\omega^{I}{}_{K}\wedge\omega^{KJ}
and the pair
F_{A}{}^{B}
and
\tilde{F}_{A^{\prime}}{}^{B^{\prime}}
with

F_{A}{}^{B}=\mathrm{d}A_{A}{}^{B}+A_{A}{}^{C}\wedge A_{C}{}^{B},\qquad\tilde{F}_{A^{\prime}}{}^{B^{\prime}}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{A}_{A^{\prime}}{}^{B^{\prime}}+\tilde{A}_{A^{\prime}}{}^{C^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{A}_{C^{\prime}}{}^{B^{\prime}},

(47a)

F_{AA^{\prime}BB^{\prime}}=F_{AB}\epsilon_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}+\tilde{F}_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\epsilon_{AB}.

(47b)

The Einstein–Cartan action can be rewritten as an
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
gauge theory with complex variables:

S=\frac{1}{16\pi G}\int_{M}\epsilon_{IJKL}e^{I}\wedge e^{J}\wedge F^{KL}_{\omega}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{16\pi G}\int_{M}e_{AA^{\prime}}\wedge e_{BB^{\prime}}\wedge(F^{AB}\epsilon^{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}-\tilde{F}^{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\epsilon^{AB}).

(48)

The two terms in the right-hand side correspond to the self-dual and the anti-self-dual sectors of gravity. In this formulation, the primed spinors transform in a complex conjugate representation to that of the unprimed ones.

Equation (
43
) implies that the soldering forms must be anti-hermitian matrices for the metric tensor to be real. In the same way, the reality of the connection 1-form
\mathchoice{\omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{J}}}}{\omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{J}}}}{\omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{J}}}}{\omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{J}}}}
implies that the self-dual and anti-self-dual connections
A
and
\tilde{A}
must be complex conjugate of each other
37
. These conditions guarantee that the two terms in the r.h.s. of (
48
) are the complex conjugate of each other and that the action is real. Thus, we start with a complex phase space consisting of two disjoint sectors and restore the real gravitational degrees of freedom by imposing the reality conditions—the anti-hermicity of the soldering form—later.

Let us introduce a basis
o_{A}
,
\iota_{A}
in the spinor space, which satisfies

\displaystyle o_{A}\iota^{A}=1,

\displaystyle o_{A}o^{A}=0=\iota_{A}\iota^{A},

(49)

and their Hermitian conjugates
\bar{o}_{A^{\prime}}
,
\bar{\iota}_{A^{\prime}}
. The totally anti-symmetric tensor can be rewritten as

\epsilon_{AB}=o_{A}\iota_{B}-\iota_{A}o_{B}.

(50)

This suggests the identification of columns and rows of the soldering form in the action (
48
) as separate unprimed and prime spinor-valued 1-forms:

\displaystyle\psi_{A}

\displaystyle=e_{AA^{\prime}}\bar{\iota}^{A^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=e_{AA^{\prime}}\iota^{A},

(51a)

\displaystyle\phi_{A}

\displaystyle=-e_{AA^{\prime}}\bar{o}^{A^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-e_{AA^{\prime}}o^{A}.

(51b)

This brings the action to the form

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}g^{-1}\int(\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge F^{AB}-\tilde{\psi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\phi}_{B^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{F}^{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}})

(52a)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}g^{-1}\int(\psi^{A}\wedge\nabla^{2}\phi_{A}-\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{A^{\prime}}),

(52b)

where we define
g
in terms of Newton’s constant as

g=8\pi G.

(53)

Integration by part and addition of the boundary action

S_{\partial}=\mathrm{i}g^{-1}\int_{\partial M}(\psi^{A}\wedge\nabla\phi_{A}-\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\phi}_{A^{\prime}})

(54)

result in the action proposed by Tung and Jacobson
45
,

S=ig^{-1}\int_{M}(\nabla\psi^{A}\wedge\nabla\phi_{A}-\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\phi}_{A^{\prime}}).

(55)

The first-order action for such a theory can be split into an anti-self-dual and self-dual parts:

S=S_{SD}+S_{ASD}.

(56)

Let
\tilde{\pi},\tilde{\rho}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\Omega^{2}(M)
, similarly to the unprimed sector. The two parts of the action take the form

\displaystyle S_{\text{SD}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\int_{M}(\pi_{A}\wedge\nabla\psi^{A}+\rho_{A}\wedge\nabla\phi^{A}+\pi_{A}\wedge\rho^{A}),

(57a)

\displaystyle S_{\text{ASD}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\int_{M}(\tilde{\pi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}+\tilde{\rho}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\phi}^{A^{\prime}}+\tilde{\pi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\rho}^{A^{\prime}}).

(57b)

To obtain the action (
39b
), we simply rescale the variables as
\psi\rightarrow\psi
,
\phi\rightarrow\phi
,
\pi\rightarrow g\pi
,
\rho\rightarrow g\rho
. For the dual sector, the analogous rescaling gives:

\displaystyle S_{\text{ASD}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\int_{M}(\tilde{\pi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}+\tilde{\rho}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\phi}^{A^{\prime}}+g\,\tilde{\pi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\rho}^{A^{\prime}}).

(58)

2.2
Equations of motion and phase space

We begin by studying the self-dual sector (
39b
), from which Robinson’s action can be obtained as a particular case in which
g=1
. In what follows, we assume
g\neq 0
and dedicate Section
4.1
to the special case of
g=0
. From the variation of action, we get the following equations of motion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,\nabla\pi_{A}\approx 0,

(59a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,\nabla\rho_{A}\approx 0,

(59b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,(g\pi_{A}-\nabla\phi_{A})\approx 0,

(59c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}_{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,(g\rho_{A}+\nabla\psi_{A})\approx 0,

(59d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,(\pi_{(A}\wedge\psi_{B)}+\rho_{(A}\wedge\phi_{B)})\approx 0.

(59e)

The constraints (
59a
)–(
59b
) and (
59c
)–(
59d
) constitute
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
-covariant versions of the 2-flatness and fake-flatness conditions, respectively, while the latter (
59e
) will be called Gauss constraint following common terminology of theories with internal Lorentz gauge symmetries. The 2-flatness constraints are also called Einstein equations in this section since this is what they correspond to in the Einstein–Cartan theory on shell of fake-flatness and Gauss constraints:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}_{A}\approx\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\nabla^{2}\phi_{A}=\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}F_{A}{}^{B}\wedge\phi_{B},

(60a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}_{A}\approx-\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\nabla^{2}\psi_{A}=-\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}F_{A}{}^{B}\wedge\psi_{B}.

(60b)

We emphasize that the Einstein equations have now their own potentials: they are expressed as total exterior derivatives of the momenta
\pi_{A}
and
\rho_{A}
.

Next, we consider the covariant phase space formalism. The symplectic potential

\Theta_{\Sigma}=\mathrm{i}\,\int_{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\psi^{A}+\rho_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi^{A}}}\right),

(61)

implies that the gravitational theory has the same kinematical phase space as the topological theory:

\Omega_{\Sigma}=\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bbvar{d}\pi_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\psi^{A}+\bbvar{d}\rho_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi^{A}}}\right).

(62)

A similar phase space in the context of gravity has been derived in
22
,
51
.

The original pre-symplectic 2-form of the self-dual-gravity phase space can be recovered up to a corner term from (
62
) by going on-shell of the fake-flatness constraint. Indeed,

\Omega\approx-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{g}\int_{\Sigma}\bbvar{d}A^{AB}\wedge\bbvar{d}(\phi_{A}\wedge\psi_{B})+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{g}\int_{C}\bbvar{d}\psi^{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi_{A}.

(63)

At this stage, the causal nature of
\Sigma
is unspecified. It might be either spacelike, timelike or null. In
Section
5
, we will restrict ourselves to the spacelike case. The case in which
\Sigma
is null implies further conditions on
\phi_{A}
and
\psi_{A}
at the boundary. If we align the spin dyad
(o^{A},\iota^{A})
to the geometry of the null boundary, these conditions simplify. They impose that the pull-back of either
\phi_{A}o^{A}
or
\psi_{A}\iota^{A}
to the boundary vanishes, depending on whether the null normal to the hypersurface is given by
\mathrm{i}\,o_{A}\bar{o}_{A^{\prime}}
or
\mathrm{i}\,\iota_{A}\bar{\iota}_{A^{\prime}}
. The resulting bulk plus boundary phase space can be then matched to earlier results developed in
50
,
52
,
51
.

Note that (
63
) is not the only possible parametrization of the on-shell phase space. The fake-flatness equations (
59c
,
59d
) can be equally well solved for connection
A
in terms of the phase-space variables:

A^{AB}_{\alpha}=\psi^{C}_{\alpha}\phi^{\mu}_{C}\psi^{\nu(A}(\pi_{\mu\nu}^{B)}-\partial_{\mu}\phi_{\nu}^{B)}+\partial_{\nu}\phi_{\mu}^{B)})-\phi^{C}_{\alpha}\psi^{\mu}_{C}\phi^{\nu(A}(\rho_{\mu\nu}^{B)}+\partial_{\mu}\psi_{\nu}^{B)}-\partial_{\nu}\psi_{\mu}^{B)}),

(64)

where we introduced the contravariant components of spinors
\psi^{\mu}
and
\phi^{\mu}
. They correspond to the frame field through the same relations as (
51
) and satisfy the following orthogonality relations:

\displaystyle\psi^{A}_{\mu}\psi^{\mu B}=0,

\displaystyle 2\psi^{A}_{(\mu}\psi_{\nu)A}=0,

(65a)

\displaystyle\phi^{A}_{\mu}\phi^{\mu B}=0,

\displaystyle 2\phi^{A}_{(\mu}\phi_{\nu)A}=0,

(65b)

\displaystyle\psi^{A}_{\mu}\phi^{\mu B}=\epsilon^{BA},

\displaystyle 2\psi^{A}_{(\mu}\phi_{\nu)A}=g_{\mu\nu},

(65c)

which, in particular, imply that the Weyl spinor–valued 4-vectors
\psi_{A}^{\mu}
,
\phi_{A}^{\mu}
are null.

Thus, the connection
A_{AB}
and area variables
\Sigma_{AB}=\phi_{(A}\wedge\psi_{B)}
of the self-dual formulation do not have to be treated as on-shell fundamental degrees of freedom. Instead, an on-shell theory can be written in terms of the line-element data, represented by
\psi_{A}
,
\phi_{A}
, and torsion-like data
\pi_{A}
,
\rho_{A}
. This choice would make our on-shell theory more akin to the teleparallel gravitation
2
,
46
.

2.3
Symmetries, charges and generator algebra

Let
\eta,\zeta\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\Omega^{1}(M)
,
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\Omega^{0}(M)
and
\alpha\in\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})\otimes\Omega^{0}(M)
. We introduce notation for the equations of motion of the self-dual sector of gravity (
59a
)–(
59e
) smeared with corresponding test functions in full analogy with the topological case:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}_{\Sigma}[\eta]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}_{A}\wedge\eta^{A}\equiv\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}(g\pi_{A}-\nabla\phi_{A})\wedge\eta^{A},

(66a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}_{\Sigma}[\zeta]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}_{A}\wedge\zeta^{A}\equiv\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}(g\rho_{A}+\nabla\psi_{A})\wedge\zeta^{A},

(66b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}_{\Sigma}[\lambda]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}^{A}\lambda_{A}\equiv\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\nabla\pi^{A}\lambda_{A},

(66c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}_{\Sigma}[\mu]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}^{A}\mu_{A}\equiv\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\nabla\rho^{A}\mu_{A},

(66d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\Sigma}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{G}_{AB}\alpha^{AB}\equiv\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}(\pi_{A}\wedge\psi_{B}+\rho_{A}\wedge\phi_{B})\alpha^{AB}.

(66e)

The gravitational action (
39b
) possesses three types of gauge symmetries: internal
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
and
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
transformations and four-dimensional diffeomorphisms. We will perform the covariant phase space analysis for the bulk phase space, equipped with the pre-symplectic 2-form (
62
) first. The corner phase space contribution will be discussed separately in
Section
3
.

\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
gauge symmetry

The
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
gauge transformations act on the spinor-valued fields infinitesimally as

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\alpha}\psi_{A}=\alpha_{A}^{\,\,\,\,B}\psi_{B},

(67a)

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\alpha}\phi_{A}=\alpha_{A}^{\,\,\,\,B}\phi_{B},

(67b)

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\alpha}\pi_{A}=\alpha_{A}^{\,\,\,\,B}\pi_{B},

(67c)

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\alpha}\rho_{A}=\alpha_{A}^{\,\,\,\,B}\rho_{B},

(67d)

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\alpha}A_{B}^{\,\,\,\,C}=-\nabla\alpha_{B}^{\,\,\,\,C}.

(67e)

Such transformations leave the action (
39b
) invariant. Its generator is given by the Gauss constraint (
66e
) and becomes trivial on-shell:

\displaystyle\bbvar{I}_{\alpha}\Omega=-\bbvar{d}H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha],

\displaystyle H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]=\mathcal{G}[\alpha].

(68)

\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
gauge symmetry

The
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
gauge symmetry is parametrized by three complex-valued scalar fields
a,b,c
on
M
, denoted collectively by
\beta
. The infinitesimal action of this symmetry on the field space is given by

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta}\psi_{A}=a\psi_{A}+b\phi_{A},

(69a)

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta}\phi_{A}=c\psi_{A}-a\phi_{A},

(69b)

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta}\pi_{A}^{\psi}=-c\rho_{A}-a\pi_{A}+\frac{1}{g}\mathrm{d}c\wedge\psi_{A}-\frac{1}{g}\mathrm{d}a\wedge\phi_{A},

(69c)

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta}\pi_{A}^{\phi}=a\rho_{A}-b\pi_{A}-\frac{1}{g}\mathrm{d}a\wedge\psi_{A}-\frac{1}{g}\mathrm{d}b\wedge\phi_{A},

(69d)

\displaystyle\delta_{\beta}A_{A}{}^{B}=0.

(69e)

The infinitesimal generator is integrable and given by a linear combination of fake-flatness constraints. The situation mirrors the topological case considered in (
26
). We obtain,

\displaystyle\bbvar{I}_{\beta}\Omega

\displaystyle=-\bbvar{d}H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta],

(70a)

\displaystyle H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{g}\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}[\delta_{\beta}\psi]+\frac{1}{g}\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}[\delta_{\beta}\phi]+q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta],

(70b)

\displaystyle q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{g}\int_{C}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c}{2}\,\psi^{A}\wedge\psi_{A}-\frac{b}{2}\,\phi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}-a\,\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}}}\right).

(70c)

The emergence of the
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
gauge transformations is easy to justify. Recall that the spinors
\psi_{A}
and
\phi_{A}
are defined as components of
e_{AA^{\prime}}
, which transform infinitesimally under
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
as

e_{AA^{\prime}}\rightarrow\beta_{A^{\prime}}{}^{B^{\prime}}e_{AB^{\prime}},\qquad\beta\in\overline{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})}.

(71)

Denoting
\beta_{0^{\prime}}{}^{0^{\prime}}=a=-\beta_{1^{\prime}}{}^{1^{\prime}}
,
\beta_{0^{\prime}}{}^{1^{\prime}}=b
,
\beta_{1^{\prime}}{}^{0^{\prime}}=c
, one arrives at the expressions (
69a
)–(
69b
). Equations (
69c
) and (
69d
) are then consistent with the equations of motion (
59c
)–(
59d
). The reader might wonder why we do not use the straightforward transformation
g\,\delta_{\beta}\pi=\nabla(\delta_{\beta}\phi)
and
g\,\delta_{\beta}\rho=-\nabla(\delta_{\beta}\psi)
instead. The reason is, however, that the Hamiltonian flow equation for this transformation is not integrable off-shell.

Diffeomorphisms

The diffeomorphisms act on fields by Lie derivation
\delta^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\xi}\equiv L_{\xi}
and leave the action invariant up to the boundary term,

\displaystyle R[\xi]

\displaystyle=\iota_{\xi}L.

(72)

The generator computed via the Noether theorem for an arbitrary vector field
\xi\in\Gamma(TM)
is given by

H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]\overset{\eqref{Noether1}}{=}\operatorname{diff}[\xi]+q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],

(73)

where the off-shell diffeomorphism generator includes each of the functionals (
66a
)–(
66e
) with field-dependent parameters, to compare with the topological case (
21a
):

\operatorname{diff}[\xi]=\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]-\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]-\mathcal{G}[\iota_{\xi}A].

(74)

The corner charge is the same as that in the topological theory:

q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]=\mathrm{i}\int_{C}(\iota_{\xi}\psi^{A}\pi_{A}+\iota_{\xi}\phi^{A}\rho_{A})\approx\frac{\mathrm{i}}{g}\int_{C}\psi^{A}\wedge{\cal L}_{\xi}\phi_{A},

(75)

where
{\cal L}_{\xi}=\nabla\iota_{\xi}+\iota_{\xi}\nabla
is the
covariant
Lie derivative. However, similarly to the topological case, the Hamiltonian flow equation is integrable only if
\xi|_{\Sigma}\in T\Sigma
and
\xi|_{C}\in T{C}
. It then takes the form

\bbvar{I}_{\xi}\Omega=-\bbvar{d}H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],

(76)

where the generator
H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]
is defined in (
73
). On-shell, this equation is satisfied if
\xi|_{C}\in T{C}
, and the generator simplifies to
q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]
.

Poisson algebra

So far, we identified different momentum maps. The resulting Poisson algebra is calculated using (
30
) as usual. Off-shell momentum maps are algebra anti-homomorphisms:

\displaystyle\{H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha_{1}],H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha_{2}]\}=-H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha],H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]\}=0,

(77a)

\displaystyle\{H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta_{1}],H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta_{2}]\}=-H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[[\beta_{1},\beta_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]\}=-H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[L_{\xi}\alpha],

(77b)

\displaystyle\{H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\chi]\}=-H^{di\!f\!\!f}[[\xi,\chi]],

\displaystyle\{H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]\}=-H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[L_{\xi}\beta].

(77c)

Closure conditions are identical to those for integrability:
\xi,\chi|_{\Sigma}\in T\Sigma
and
\xi,\chi|_{C}\in T{C}
.

Shift generators

Recall that in the topological theory we were able to decompose the
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
and diffeomorphism corner charges in terms of the shift charges (see (
27
,
22a
)). Moreover, the same held true for off-shell generators (
21a
,
26
). That diffeomorphisms are on shell equivalent to a combination of field-dependent shift and Lorentz transformations has been long known in the context of three-dimensional gravity
1
,
54
. More recent developments extended this statement to gravity in four dimensions
34
,
22
,
31
. The interest in this is driven, in particular, by an observation that shifts are more convenient for discretization and quantization
23
,
16
,
12
. This viewpoint is especially natural in formulations of gravity as deformed topological theories, of which our theory is an example. Then, diffeomorphisms represent the subset of shifts left unbroken by the deformation. In our formulation, this is also the case with the
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
gauge.

While the meaning of the decompositions (
21a
,
26
) is obvious in the topological theory, the meaning of individual terms in the expressions (
70b
,
74
) of
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
and diffeomorphism generators in self-dual gravity has not been yet discussed. As one might infer by analogy, the smeared equations of motion on
\Sigma
(
66a
)–(
66d
) generate shift transformations of phase-space variables, with the only difference being that
\mathrm{d}
-exact shifts become
\nabla
-exact. However, shifts are not a symmetry of the gravitational action (with the exception of diffeomorphisms and
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
transformations), and their generators require a phase-space extension to be integrable. In the remainder of this section, we provide such an extension, which then allows us to introduce shift generators (
66a
)–(
66b
) as phase-space symmetry generators in the interior of
\Sigma
and justify an interpretation of
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
and diffeomorphism transformations as field-dependent shifts from the intrinsically gravitational point of view. At the level of the corner charges, this interpretation translates into the decompositions (
22a
,
27
).

Let
\Pi_{A}{}^{B}
be an
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
-valued 2-form. Consider an extended symplectic form

\Omega^{ext}_{\Sigma}=\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bbvar{d}\pi_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\psi^{A}+\bbvar{d}\rho_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi^{A}+\bbvar{d}\Pi_{AB}\wedge\bbvar{d}A^{AB}}}\right).

(78)

\Pi_{AB}
plays the role of the conjugate momentum to the connection
A^{AB}
. In order not to affect the physical phase space, we require that
\Pi_{AB}
vanishes on shell. This phase space corresponds to the action (
39b
) extended by a term

\mathrm{i}\int_{M}\Pi_{AB}(F^{AB}-\gamma^{AB}).

(79)

A similar extension will be discussed in the context of canonical analysis in
Section
5
. The extra equations of motion are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{AB}+\nabla\Pi_{AB}=0,\qquad F_{AB}=\gamma_{AB},\qquad\Pi_{AB}=0.

(80)

We now move on to the discussion of symmetries. Let
\eta_{A},\zeta_{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\Omega^{1}(M)
and
\lambda_{A},\mu_{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\Omega^{0}(M)
. Consider the shift transformation

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}\psi_{A}

\displaystyle=g\,\eta_{A},

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\delta_{\eta}\pi_{A}

\displaystyle=0,

(81a)

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}\phi_{A}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}\rho_{A}

\displaystyle=-\nabla\eta_{A},

(81b)

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}A_{AB}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}\Pi_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\phi_{(A}\wedge\eta_{B)}.

(81c)

The Hamiltonian flow equation for such a transformation is integrable off-shell:

\displaystyle\bbvar{I}_{\eta}\Omega

\displaystyle=-\bbvar{d}{\cal H}_{\phi}[\eta],

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{\phi}[\eta]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\phi}[\eta]+q_{\phi}[\eta],

(82)

where
\mathcal{F}_{\phi}[\eta]
is defined in (
66a
), and
q_{\phi}[\eta]
is the same as in the topological case, (
15a
). Analogously, the shift transformation with non-trivial components

\displaystyle\delta_{\zeta}\phi_{A}

\displaystyle=g\,\zeta_{A},

(83a)

\displaystyle\delta_{\zeta}\pi_{A}

\displaystyle=\nabla\zeta_{A},

(83b)

\displaystyle\delta_{\eta}\Pi_{AB}

\displaystyle=\psi_{(A}\wedge\zeta_{B)}

(83c)

is generated by

{\cal H}_{\psi}[\zeta]=\mathcal{F}_{\psi}[\zeta]+q_{\psi}[\zeta].

(84)

The covariantly-exact shifts with non-trivial components

\displaystyle\delta_{\lambda}\psi_{A}

\displaystyle=\nabla\lambda_{A},

and

\displaystyle\hskip 25.00003pt\delta_{\mu}\phi_{A}

\displaystyle=\nabla\mu_{A},

\displaystyle\delta_{\lambda}\Pi_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{(A}\pi_{B)}

and

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}\Pi_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{(A}\rho_{B)},

(85)

are generated, respectively, by

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{\pi}[\lambda]=\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\lambda]+q_{\pi}[\lambda],

(86a)

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{\rho}[\mu]=\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu]+q_{\rho}[\mu].

(86b)

We note that corner shift generators on the gravitational phase space coincide with the topological charges (
15
,
20
). Thus, while
q_{\phi}
and
q_{\psi}
are not corner charges, they still appear in the theory of the self-dual gravity with an extended phase space as corner shift generators. In the next section, we will use this decomposition to show that the corner shift generators can be switched off with a certain corner phase-space extension.

2.4
Cosmological constant

There are three different ways to rewrite the cosmological constant contribution in the spinor variables,

\displaystyle S_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16\pi G}\int\frac{\lambda}{6}\epsilon_{IJKL}e^{I}\wedge e^{J}\wedge e^{K}\wedge e^{L}=

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda}{12g}\int\epsilon^{AA^{\prime}BB^{\prime}CC^{\prime}DD^{\prime}}e_{AA^{\prime}}\wedge e_{BB^{\prime}}\wedge e_{CC^{\prime}}\wedge e_{DD^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\Lambda}{2g}\int\psi_{A}\wedge\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge\phi^{B}

(87a)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\Lambda}{4g}\int(\psi_{A}\wedge\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge\phi^{B}-\tilde{\psi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\phi}_{B^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\phi}^{B^{\prime}})

(87b)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}\Lambda}{2g}\int\tilde{\psi}_{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\psi}^{A^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\phi}_{B^{\prime}}\wedge\tilde{\phi}^{B^{\prime}}.

(87c)

It is interesting that the cosmological constant contribution can be distributed symmetrically between the self-dual and anti-self-dual sectors (
87b
), or concentrated fully in one of them, see (
87a
) or (
87c
). All of these options will match upon imposing the reality conditions, studied in
Section
3
.

Assume the choice (
87a
). While fake-flatness and Gauss equations of motion are not modified, the Einstein equation acquires a cosmological-constant term,

\displaystyle\nabla\pi^{\psi}_{A}

\displaystyle+\frac{\Lambda}{g}\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge\phi^{B}=0,

(88a)

\displaystyle\nabla\pi^{\phi}_{A}

\displaystyle+\frac{\Lambda}{g}\phi_{A}\wedge\psi_{B}\wedge\psi^{B}=0.

(88b)

This means that the following momentum maps are modified in the case
\Lambda\neq 0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}_{\Lambda}[\lambda]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}(\nabla\pi^{A}+\frac{\Lambda}{g}\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge\phi^{B})\lambda_{A},

(89a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}_{\Lambda}[\mu]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}(\nabla\rho^{A}+\frac{\Lambda}{g}\phi^{A}\wedge\psi_{B}\wedge\psi^{B})\mu_{A}.

(89b)

The generator of diffeomorphisms, which is the only generator containing
{\cal E}
-momentum maps, is integrable on-shell if
\xi|_{C}\in TC
, and off-shell if also
\xi|_{\Sigma}\in T\Sigma
. If
\xi_{\Sigma}\in T\Sigma
,

\displaystyle H^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\Lambda}[\xi]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{diff}_{\Lambda}[\xi]+q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],

(90a)

\displaystyle\operatorname{diff}_{\Lambda}[\xi]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{\pi}{}_{\Lambda}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}_{\Lambda}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]-\mathcal{F}_{\phi}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+\mathcal{F}_{\psi}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]-\mathcal{G}[\iota_{\xi}A].

(90b)

However, if
\xi
has a time-like component, the on-shell generator does not vanish in the interior of
\Sigma
but contains a volume-form contribution:

H^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\Lambda}[\xi]\approx-\frac{\mathrm{i}\Lambda}{2g}\int_{\Sigma}\iota_{\xi}(\psi_{A}\wedge\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge\phi^{B})+q^{di\!f\!\!f}.

(91)

If
\xi|_{\Sigma}\in T\Sigma
and
\xi|_{C}\in TC
, the Poisson algebra of diffeomorphism generators is analogous to the
\Lambda=0
case (
77
).

3
The corner phase space ambiguity

In field theories, there are two major ambiguities in the definition of a symplectic potential. First, we can always add a total field-space variation, thus shifting
\Theta
into
\Theta-\bbvar{d}F
, where
F
is a generating functional, corresponding to a canonical transformation of the canonical phase space coordinates. At the classical level, such canonical transformations do not change the physics. At the quantum level they may, because not every canonical transformation is also represented as a unitary map on the Hilbert space
29
. In other words, different choices of canonical variables can correspond to unitarily inequivalent quantum theories. Second, the variation of the action fixes the pre-symplectic potential only up to a corner term—the symplectic current (
63
) is defined only up to a
\mathrm{d}
-exact 3-form. The addition of such
\mathrm{d}
-exact 3-forms adds a corner term to the symplectic potential. What are the possible such corner terms? If we only allow terms that are polynomial in the fundamental fields, the two non-trivial gauge-covariant
(2,1)
-forms, i.e.
(2,1)
-forms that are invariant under the internal
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
action and under diffeomorphisms whose generators are tangent to the corner, are then given by

\psi_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi^{A},\qquad A_{AB}\wedge\bbvar{d}A^{AB}.

(92)

This defines the most general polynomial ambiguity of the corner symplectic potential. The resulting symplectic form consists, therefore, of two terms parametrized by two
complex-valued
coupling constants
\gamma,\nu\in\mathbb{C}
:

\displaystyle\Theta^{C}_{\gamma}=\mathrm{i}(\gamma g)^{-1}\int_{C}\psi_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi^{A},

\displaystyle\Omega^{C}_{\gamma}=\mathrm{i}(\gamma g)^{-1}\int_{C}\bbvar{d}\psi_{A}\wedge\bbvar{d}\phi^{A},

(93a)

\displaystyle\Theta^{C}_{\nu}=-\mathrm{i}\nu\int_{C}A_{AB}\wedge\bbvar{d}A^{AB},

\displaystyle\Omega^{C}_{\nu}=-\mathrm{i}\nu\int_{C}\bbvar{d}A_{AB}\wedge\bbvar{d}A^{AB},

(93b)

with
\gamma\rightarrow\infty,\,\nu\rightarrow 0
corresponding to the phase space (
62
), and
\gamma=1,\,\nu=0
to the self-dual gravity phase space as can be easily seen from (
63
). Such an extension matches exactly the phase space contribution found in the literature due to the Holst and Chern class topological terms if
\gamma
is related to the Immirzi parameter
\gamma_{\text{Im}}
as
\gamma_{\text{Im}}=\mathrm{i}\gamma
(see
20
,
24
,
26
). The corner phase space of the kind (
93a
,
93b
) was obtained also in
18
from the MacDowell-Mansouri action. In our case, however, the constants
\gamma
and
\nu
are treated independently. Implications of such a modification on the corner charge algebra are discussed in this section.

3.1
Holst term

First, we focus on the Holst term ambiguity parameterized by
\gamma
.
As a reminder, the following on-shell corner charges, corresponding to the symmetries of the action (
39b
), were obtained in (
68
), (
70c
), and (
75
):

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=0,

(94a)

\displaystyle q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\!\!\int_{C}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c}{2}\,\psi^{A}\wedge\psi_{A}-\frac{b}{2}\,\phi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}-a\,\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}}}\right),

(94b)

\displaystyle q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,\int_{C}(\iota_{\xi}\psi^{A}\pi_{A}+\iota_{\xi}\phi^{A}\rho_{A}).

(94c)

The on-shell corner-charge algebra follows the same pattern as the off-shell generator algebra (
77
). The vanishing
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
charge makes some of the Poisson brackets trivially zero on shell. For any diffeomorphism parameter
\xi
, such that
\xi|_{C}\in T{C}
,

\displaystyle\{q^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha_{1}],q^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha_{2}]\}\equiv 0,

\displaystyle\{q^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha],q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]\}\equiv 0,

(95a)

\displaystyle\{q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta_{1}],q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta_{2}]\}=-q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[[\beta_{1},\beta_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],q^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]\}\equiv 0,

(95b)

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi_{1}],q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi_{2}]\}=-q^{di\!f\!\!f}[[\xi_{1},\xi_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi],q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]\}=-q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\partial_{\xi}\beta].

(95c)

Such a set of corner charges implies that the corresponding quantum theory will not carry a representation of
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
. However, let us remember that we can make a different choice of the corner phase space. The corner charges for a general pre-symplectic 2-form
\Omega_{\gamma}
(
93a
) modify the charges. They now read

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}(\gamma g)^{-1}\!\!\int_{C}\alpha^{AB}\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}=

(96a)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha],

(96b)

\displaystyle q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\beta]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}(1-\gamma^{-1})g^{-1}\!\int_{C}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c}{2}\,\psi^{A}\wedge\psi_{A}-\frac{b}{2}\,\phi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}-a\,\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}}}\right)=

(96c)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma^{-1})q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta],

(96d)

\displaystyle q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}(1-\gamma^{-1})\!\int_{C}(\iota_{\xi}\psi^{A}\pi_{A}+\iota_{\xi}\phi^{A}\rho_{A})-\mathrm{i}(\gamma g)^{-1}\!\!\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}A^{AB}\,\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B},

(96e)

where the notation
q_{1}
stands for
q_{\gamma=1}
and is mentioned in more detail below, while
q\equiv q_{\infty}
refers to the corner charges with a trivial corner phase space (
\gamma\rightarrow\infty
) derived in the previous section. The
\gamma
-modified corner charge algebra
\forall\xi:\xi|_{C}\in T{C}
becomes

\displaystyle\{q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha_{1}],q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha_{2}]\}=-q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha],q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\beta]\}=0,

(97a)

\displaystyle\{q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\beta_{1}],q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\beta_{2}]\}=-q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[[\beta_{1},\beta_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi],q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha]\}=-q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\partial_{\xi}\alpha],

(97b)

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi_{1}],q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi_{2}]\}=-q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[[\xi_{1},\xi_{2}]],

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi],q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\beta]\}=-q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\partial_{\xi}\beta],

(97c)

which provides a possibility for a richer representation theory.

The choice of
\gamma=1
(
\gamma_{\text{Im}}=\mathrm{i}
) restores the self-dual gravity phase space. It leads to a swap of a non-trivial charge from
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
to
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
, as well as a change in the diffeomorphism charge:

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\int_{C}\alpha^{AB}\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B},

(98a)

\displaystyle q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{1}[\beta]

\displaystyle=0,

(98b)

\displaystyle q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{1}[\xi]

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\,g^{-1}\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}A^{AB}\,\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}.

(98c)

However, to understand this at a deeper level, let us rewrite (
96e
) using the charge decompositions (
22a
,
27
) to single out Lorentz and shift components of corner generators:

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha],

(99a)

\displaystyle q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\beta]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\gamma^{-1}}{2g}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{\phi}[\delta_{\beta}\psi^{A}]+q_{\psi}[\delta_{\beta}\phi_{A}]}}\right)

(99b)

\displaystyle q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi]

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\gamma^{-1}}{2g}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-q_{\phi}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+q_{\psi}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]}}\right)-\gamma^{-1}q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\iota_{\xi}A].

(99c)

This suggests that
\gamma
is a switch between
Lorentz
and
shift
generator components. Namely,
\gamma=\infty
corresponds to a non-zero shift generator contribution and a trivial
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
contribution, whereas
\gamma=1
enables
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
charges and makes shift components across all charges vanish.

Note that the phase-space extension (
93a
) can be added to the topological phase space (
10
) as well. Since the connection is absent from the theory, such corner term would simply cancel the corner charges whenever
\gamma=1
.

For completeness, we mention that off-shell diffeomorphisms generators
H_{\gamma}
obtained from
H
via the deformation
q\rightarrow q_{\gamma}
of their corner components satisfy the same algebra (
77
). Off-shell, the corner part of the diffeomorphism charge, instead of (
96e
), becomes more involved:

q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi]=\mathrm{i}\int_{C}(\iota_{\xi}\psi^{A}(\pi_{A}-(\gamma g)^{-1}\nabla\phi_{A})+\iota_{\xi}\phi^{A}(\rho_{A}+(\gamma g)^{-1}\nabla\psi_{A}))-i(\gamma g)^{-1}\!\!\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}A^{AB}\,\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}.

(100)

It can be simplified to (
96e
), in particular, by imposing the boundary conditions
\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}^{A}|_{C}=0
and
\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}^{A}|_{C}=0
(see (
59c
), (
59d
)) at
{C}
. Note that the addition of the corner phase space (
93a
) in the
g=0
case is impossible since it would diverge in the limit.

3.2
Chern–Simons corner charges

The Chern-Simons corner charge contribution corresponding to the
symmetries of the bulk action
is easily integrable. First let us consider the gauge transformations, with
\alpha\in\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
a field-independent parameter:

\bbvar{I}_{\alpha}\Omega^{C}_{\nu}=-\bbvar{d}q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\alpha],\qquad q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\alpha]=\mathrm{i}\nu\int_{C}\alpha^{AB}F_{AB}.

(101)

Similarly, charges of diffeomorphisms generated by a field-independent vector field
\xi
are integrable if
\xi|_{C}\in T{C}
:

\bbvar{I}_{\xi}\Omega^{C}_{\nu}=-\bbvar{d}q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\alpha],\qquad q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\nu}[\alpha]=-\mathrm{i}\nu\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}A^{AB}dA_{AB}.

(102)

Note that via the addition of

-\mathrm{i}\nu\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}A^{AB}A_{A}{}^{C}\wedge A_{CB}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\nu}{3}\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}(A^{AB}\wedge A_{A}{}^{C}\wedge A_{CB})\overset{\xi|_{C}\in T{C}}{=}0

(103)

the charge can be brought into a nice form:

q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\nu}[\alpha]=-\mathrm{i}\nu\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}A^{AB}F_{AB}=-q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\iota_{\xi}A],

(104)

which matches the charge relation in the rest of the analysis. The Chern–Simons charges satisfy the already familiar algebra:

\displaystyle\{q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\alpha_{1}],q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\alpha_{2}]\}=-q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}]],

(105a)

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\nu}[\xi],q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\alpha]\}=-q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\nu}[\partial_{\xi}\alpha],

(105b)

\displaystyle\{q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\nu}[\xi_{1}],q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\nu}[\xi_{2}]\}=-q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\nu}[[\xi_{1},\xi_{2}]].

(105c)

The Chern–Simons charges do not affect the other corner generators like Holst contributions do; hence, we end their discussion here. Related developments have been reported in
27
,
18
,
32
.

3.3
The dual sector

Due to the similarity of the actions (
39b
) and (
58
), the analysis above can be straightforwardly extended to the dual sector. We proceed as follows.
Let
\tilde{f}[\alpha]
denote a functional that is equal to
f[\alpha]
up to the substitution
\psi\rightarrow\tilde{\psi}
,
\phi\rightarrow\tilde{\phi}
,
\pi\rightarrow\tilde{\pi}
,
\rho\rightarrow\tilde{\rho}
and an overall minus sign. Then,

\tilde{\Omega}_{\Sigma}=-\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bbvar{d}\tilde{\pi}_{{A^{\prime}}}\wedge\bbvar{d}\tilde{\psi}^{{A^{\prime}}}+\bbvar{d}\tilde{\rho}_{{A^{\prime}}}\wedge\bbvar{d}\tilde{\phi}^{{A^{\prime}}}}}\right),

(106)

and the considerations above follow through without any change according to the following formulas:

\bbvar{I}_{\xi}\tilde{\Omega}_{\Sigma}=-\bbvar{d}\tilde{H}_{\Sigma}[\xi],

(107)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{G}}[\alpha],

(108a)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{g}{}^{\tilde{\psi}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\delta_{\beta}\tilde{\psi}]+\frac{1}{g}{}^{\tilde{\phi}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\delta_{\beta}\tilde{\phi}]+\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta],

(108b)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]

\displaystyle={}^{\tilde{\psi}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\iota_{\xi}\tilde{\psi}]+{}^{\tilde{\phi}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}[\iota_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}]-{}^{\tilde{\psi}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\iota_{\xi}\tilde{\rho}]+{}^{\tilde{\phi}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\iota_{\xi}\tilde{\pi}]-\tilde{\mathcal{G}}[\iota_{\xi}\tilde{A}]+\tilde{q}^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi].

(108c)

The tilde-sector charges satisfy the same algebra (
77
) and (
97
). Note that if the current variables are to be treated as fundamental, the corner phase space of the dual sector can
a priori
have a different Immirzi parameter
4
4

4

The choice of the opposite sign in this relation in contrast to the unprimed sector is due to the Holst terms of the two sectors having equal signs:

S=-(g\gamma_{\text{Im}})^{-1}\int_{M}e_{I}\wedge e_{J}\wedge F^{IJ}_{\omega}=-2(g\gamma_{\text{Im}})^{-1}\int_{M}(\psi_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}\wedge F^{AB}+\tilde{\psi}_{A}\wedge\tilde{\phi}_{B}\wedge\tilde{F}^{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}})
.

\tilde{\gamma}_{\text{Im}}=-\mathrm{i}\tilde{\gamma}
,

\tilde{\Omega}_{\Sigma}+\tilde{\Omega}^{C}_{\tilde{\gamma}}=-\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta\tilde{\pi}_{{A^{\prime}}}\wedge\delta\tilde{\psi}^{{A^{\prime}}}+\delta\tilde{\rho}_{{A^{\prime}}}\wedge\delta\tilde{\phi}^{{A^{\prime}}}}}\right)-\mathrm{i}(\tilde{\gamma}g)^{-1}\int_{C}\delta\tilde{\psi}_{{A^{\prime}}}\wedge\delta\tilde{\phi}^{{A^{\prime}}}.

(109)

The Chern–Simons extension can also be added to the dual sector. However, we will not discuss it in what follows.

3.4
Corner charges under reality conditions

Let us now study the impact of the reality conditions on the charges in the presence of both the self-dual and anti-self-dual sectors of gravity. Owing to the decoupling of the two sectors in (
56
) and linearity of the vector operations applied in the covariant phase space analysis, the total gravitational symplectic form, symmetry generators, and charges are simply a sum of contributions from the two sectors. This, however, no longer holds, if we go on-shell of the reality conditions, which relate the variables in the two sectors.

We recall that the soldering form can be decomposed into either unprimed or primed spinors:

e_{AA^{\prime}}=\psi_{A}\bar{o}_{A^{\prime}}+\phi_{A}\bar{\iota}_{A^{\prime}},

(110a)

e_{AA^{\prime}}=\tilde{\psi}_{A^{\prime}}o_{A}+\tilde{\phi}_{A^{\prime}}\iota_{A}.

(110b)

A pseudo-Riemannian tetrad with a signature
(-,+,+,+)
corresponds to an anti-hermitian soldering form (see Section
2.1
):

e_{AA^{\prime}}^{\dagger}=-e_{AA^{\prime}}.

(111)

We can obtain two kinds of reality conditions on the phase space variables from this equation. The first set of conditions can be obtained via the substitution of (
110a
) into (
111
). These guarantee reality of a tetrad defined by the soldering form (
110a
)
40
:

\displaystyle(\psi_{0})^{*}

\displaystyle=-\psi_{0},

(112a)

\displaystyle(\phi_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle=-\phi_{1},

(112b)

\displaystyle(\psi_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle=-\phi_{0},

(112c)

where
(a)^{\ast}
is the complex conjugate of
a\in\mathbb{C}
.
Analogously, substitution of (
110b
) into (
111
) leads to

\displaystyle(\tilde{\psi}_{0})^{*}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\psi}_{0},

(113a)

\displaystyle(\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\phi}_{1},

(113b)

\displaystyle(\tilde{\psi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\phi}_{0}.

(113c)

The second kind of conditions is obtained upon substitution of (
110a
) and (
110b
) into the right- and left-hand sides of (
111
) respectively. This yields consistency of the definitions (
110a
) and (
110b
) and compares fields from opposite sectors:

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\psi^{\dagger}_{A^{\prime}},

(114a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\phi^{\dagger}_{A^{\prime}}.

(114b)

The Hermitian conjugate of a two-component spinor is defined as an anti-linear map
{}^{\dagger}:\mathbb{C}^{2}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{C}^{2}}
and
\overline{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2}
, which preserves the anti-symmetric tensor,
(\epsilon_{AB})^{\dagger}=\epsilon_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda^{\dagger}_{A}

\displaystyle:=(\lambda_{A^{\prime}})^{*},

(115a)

\displaystyle\mu^{\dagger}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=(\mu_{A})^{*}.

(115b)

The reality conditions are not preserved by either
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
or
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
transformations. Requiring that a joint
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
and
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
gauge transformation preserves (
112
) (or, equivalently, (
111
)),

(\delta_{\alpha}+\delta_{\beta})\big(\psi_{0}+(\psi_{0})^{*}\big)=0,\quad\text{etc.,}

(116)

leads to a relationship between the two gauge algebra parameters:

\beta=\alpha^{*}.

(117)

This restores the conjugacy relationship not only at the level of phase space fields, but also at the level of the gauge transformations.

An explicit computation shows that the following relations
5
5

5

We recall the notation
q\equiv q_{\infty}
.
can be established between the corner charges on shell of the conditions (
112
)–(
114
):

\displaystyle q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha^{*}]

\displaystyle\overset{\cdot}{\approx}(q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha])^{*},

(118a)

\displaystyle\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha]

\displaystyle\overset{\cdot}{\approx}(\tilde{q}^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha^{*}])^{*},

(118b)

\displaystyle\tilde{q}^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha^{*}]

\displaystyle\overset{\cdot}{\approx}(q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha])^{*},

(118c)

\displaystyle\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha]

\displaystyle\overset{\cdot}{\approx}(q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha^{*}])^{*}.

(118d)

Here, and in the rest of this section, the symbol
\overset{\cdot}{\approx}
is reserved to denote application of the reality conditions. We recall that we considered the possibility that a different factor
\tilde{\gamma}
could encode the corner ambiguity for the tilde variables sector. Putting the two sectors together with the reality conditions, we obtain different relations for the corner charges:

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha]+q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\alpha^{*}]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha^{*}]+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{\gamma}\operatorname{Im}(q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha]),

(119a)

\displaystyle\tilde{q}^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\tilde{\gamma}}[\alpha^{*}]+\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\tilde{\gamma}}[\alpha]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha]+\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{\tilde{\gamma}}\operatorname{Im}(\tilde{q}^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha^{*}])\overset{\cdot}{\approx}(q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha^{*}])^{*}-\frac{2\mathrm{i}}{\tilde{\gamma}}\operatorname{Im}(q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha]),

(119b)

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\gamma}[\alpha]+q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\gamma}[\alpha^{*}]+\tilde{q}^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{\tilde{\gamma}}[\alpha^{*}]+\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\tilde{\gamma}}[\alpha]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}2\operatorname{Re}(q_{1}^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha])+2\mathrm{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\gamma}-\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}}\right)\operatorname{Im}(q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha])=

(119c)

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma}{=}2\operatorname{Re}(q^{\mathfrak{sl}}_{1}[\alpha])\overset{\cdot}{\approx}2\operatorname{Re}(q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha^{*}]).

(119d)

Thus, the extended corner phase space (
93a
) does not affect the total
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
charge of the real on-shell theory if the Immirzi parameters
\gamma,\,\tilde{\gamma}
of the two sectors match. If only the real parts of the inverse Immirzi parameters are equal,

\operatorname{Re}\frac{1}{\gamma}=\operatorname{Re}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}},

(120)

the total corner charge is real and contains an Immirzi-dependent contribution. If

\operatorname{Re}\frac{1}{\gamma}\neq\operatorname{Re}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}},

(121)

the extension controls the imaginary contribution of the
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
on-shell charge. Thus, matching real parts of the inverse Immirzi parameters are necessary for the reality of the corner charges. In the case
\gamma,\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}
, the requirement that the corner charges be real simplifies to
\gamma=\tilde{\gamma}
.

Similarly, diffeomorphism corner charges add up to a real corner charge on shell of the reality conditions. It is easy to see that momenta
\pi
and
\rho
follow the same reality conditions as the spinors
\psi
and
\phi
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}^{\dagger}_{A}

\displaystyle=-\pi_{A},

(122a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}^{\dagger}_{A}

\displaystyle=-\rho_{A}.

(122b)

Taking this into account, we obtain

q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi]+\tilde{q}^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\tilde{\gamma}}[\xi]=q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]+(q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi])^{*}+\frac{1}{\gamma}q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{1}[\xi]+\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}(q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{1}[\xi])^{*}=\\
=2\operatorname{Re}q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\gamma}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}}\right)\operatorname{Re}(q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{1}[\xi])+\mathrm{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\gamma}-\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}}\right)\operatorname{Im}(q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{1}[\xi])\overset{\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma}{=}2\operatorname{Re}q^{di\!f\!\!f}_{\gamma}[\xi].

(123)

The reality of the diffeomorphism charge, in addition to (
120
), also requires

\operatorname{Im}\frac{1}{\gamma}=-\operatorname{Im}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}.

(124)

Hence, the reality of corner charges implies

\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma^{*}\qquad\iff\qquad\tilde{\gamma}_{\text{Im}}=\gamma^{*}_{\text{Im}}.

(125)

At last, on shell of the reality conditions, full shift charges are restored in (
27
) and (
22a
):

\displaystyle q^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]+q^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha^{*}]+\tilde{q}^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha^{*}]+\tilde{q}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\alpha]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}\frac{1}{g}\operatorname{Re}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{\phi}[\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\alpha^{*}}\psi]+q_{\psi}[\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\alpha^{*}}\phi]}}\right),

(126a)

\displaystyle q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]+\tilde{q}^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}-q_{\phi}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+q_{\psi}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]+q_{\pi}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]+q_{\rho}[\iota_{\xi}\phi].

(126b)

This permits a concise off-shell expression

\displaystyle H^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta]+\tilde{H}^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{*}]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}\frac{1}{g}\operatorname{Re}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(H_{\phi}[\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\alpha^{*}}\psi]+H_{\psi}[\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\alpha^{*}}\phi]}}\right),

(127a)

\displaystyle H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]+\tilde{H}^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]\overset{\cdot}{\approx}

(127b)

\displaystyle\overset{\cdot}{\approx}\operatorname{Re}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-H_{\phi}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+H_{\psi}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]+H_{\pi}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]+H_{\rho}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]-H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\iota_{\xi}A]}}\right),

(127c)

where shift generators in the topological case are defined as in (
13
,
17
), while in the gravity case they are given by (
82
,
84
,
86
) and defined on an extended phase space.

4
Weak-coupling limit

Thanks to the new first-order formulation in terms of the spinor variables, we can perform in a new manner the limit
G\propto g\rightarrow 0
by rescaling the momentum variables.
At the topological level, this corresponds to dealing with
skeletal
2-groups
6
,
13
as symmetries. At the gravitational level, we obtain a new theory, physical aspects of which still need to be understood.

4.1
The
g\rightarrow 0
limit of the topological theory

Let us revisit the analysis performed in
Section
1
but with
g=0
now. It has certain distinct features despite an algebraic smoothness of the limit. First, the momenta become dynamically independent of the configuration variables. Second, the exact shifts become an independent symmetry transformation.

The action has in this case one term less. It now reads:

S_{0}=\mathrm{i}\int_{M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{A}\wedge\mathrm{d}\psi^{A}+\rho_{A}\wedge\mathrm{d}\phi^{A}}}\right),

(128)

but the off-shell phase space remains the same as before.

Let us count the dynamical degrees of freedom. In the case
g=0
, the two pairs of equations (
8a
)–(
8b
) and (
8c
)–(
8d
) disentangle,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\psi_{A}=0,

(129a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\phi_{A}=0

(129b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\rho_{A}=0,

(129c)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\pi_{A}=0.

(129d)

In contrast to the topological theory (
5
) with
g\neq 0
, equations (
129c
,
129d
) are independent of equations (
129a
,
129b
) and constrain two degrees of freedom each. At the same time, since
\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}\psi_{A})\equiv 0\equiv\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}\phi_{A})
, each of the constraints (
129a
,
129b
) implies two trivial equations. Thus, the theory still has 12 non-trivial independent first-class constraints and has no local dynamical degrees of freedom.

Shift symmetry

Similarly to the constraints (
129
), the corresponding gauge transformations disentangle in the
g\rightarrow 0
limit and exhibit a 2-gauge structure. The remaining non-trivial shifts affect only the canonical moments:

\delta_{\eta}\rho_{A}=-\mathrm{d}\eta_{A}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\delta_{\zeta}\pi_{A}=\mathrm{d}\zeta_{A}.

(130)

Their corresponding generators simplify to

\displaystyle\bbvar{I}_{\eta}\Omega

\displaystyle=-\bbvar{d}H^{\prime}_{\psi}[\eta],

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{\psi}[\eta]

\displaystyle=F_{\phi}^{\prime}{}{}[\eta]+q_{\phi}[\eta],

(131a)

\displaystyle\bbvar{I}_{\zeta}\Omega

\displaystyle=-\bbvar{d}H^{\prime}_{\phi}[\eta],

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{\phi}[\eta]

\displaystyle=F_{\psi}^{\prime}[\zeta]+q_{\psi}[\zeta],

(131b)

where the new momentum maps correspond to the equations of motion in the
g\rightarrow 0
limit,

\displaystyle F_{\phi}^{\prime}[\eta]=\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{d}\phi^{A}\wedge\eta_{A},

(132a)

\displaystyle F_{\psi}^{\prime}[\zeta]=\mathrm{i}\int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{d}\psi_{A}\wedge\zeta^{A}.

(132b)

These momentum maps can be directly obtained from the limit
g\rightarrow 0
.

Gauge symmetry

The exact shifts become an independent gauge transformation generated by
H_{\pi}[\lambda]
and
H_{\rho}[\mu]
introduced in (
17
), which are not affected by the limit.

Global
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
symmetry

The global
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
transformation (
28
) is generated by a physical conserved bulk charge

H^{\mathfrak{sl}}[\alpha]=\mathrm{i}\alpha^{AB}\int_{\Sigma}(\pi_{A}\wedge\psi_{B}+\rho_{A}\wedge\phi_{B}),

(133)

since the equations of motion no longer relate spinors and their momenta. Considering the Poisson algebra of such generators gives the
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
algebra as in (
37
).

\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
local symmetry

The new realization of the
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
local symmetry is given by

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\psi_{A}=0,

(134a)

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\phi_{A}=0,

(134b)

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\pi_{A}^{\psi}=\mathrm{d}c^{\prime}\wedge\psi_{A}-\mathrm{d}a^{\prime}\wedge\phi_{A},

(134c)

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\pi_{A}^{\phi}=-\mathrm{d}a^{\prime}\wedge\psi_{A}-\mathrm{d}b^{\prime}\wedge\phi_{A}.

(134d)

Its Hamiltonian flow equation takes the form

\displaystyle\bbvar{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\Omega

\displaystyle=-\bbvar{d}H^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}],

(135a)

\displaystyle H^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=F_{\phi}{}{}^{\prime}[a\psi+b\phi]+F_{\psi}{}{}^{\prime}[c\psi-a\phi]+q^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}],

(135b)

\displaystyle q^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\int_{C}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}\,\psi^{A}\wedge\psi_{A}-\frac{b^{\prime}}{2}\,\phi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}-a^{\prime}\,\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{A}}}\right)=

(135c)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{\phi}[a^{\prime}\psi+b^{\prime}\phi]+q_{\psi}[c^{\prime}\psi-a^{\prime}\phi]}}\right).

(135d)

The notation
\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}
is due to the fact that this transformation can be obtained from
\overline{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})}
(
25
) by performing a rescaling

\beta^{\prime}=\beta/g

(136)

in (
25
)–(
27
) and setting
g=0
. Considering the Poisson algebra of such generators gives the
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
algebra as in (
34
).

Diffeomorphism symmetry

The diffeomorphism generator is integrable on shell only if
\xi|_{C}\in TC
and off shell if, additionally,
\xi|_{\Sigma}\in T\Sigma
. It can be expressed through the momentum maps (
132
):

H^{\prime di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]=-F_{\phi}^{\prime}{}{}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+F_{\psi}^{\prime}{}{}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]+E_{\pi}{}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]+E_{\rho}{}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]+q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi].

(137)

Dual diffeomorphisms

This theory is additionally symmetric under dual diffeomorphisms defined as
38
,
25

\displaystyle\delta^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}_{\chi}\psi

\displaystyle=L_{\chi}\phi,

\displaystyle\delta^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}_{\chi}\pi

\displaystyle=L_{\chi}\rho

(138a)

\displaystyle\delta^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}_{\chi}\phi

\displaystyle=L_{\chi}\psi

\displaystyle\delta^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}_{\chi}\rho

\displaystyle=L_{\chi}\pi.

(138b)

The corresponding generator is given by

H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]=-F_{\phi}^{\prime}{}{}[\iota_{\chi}\pi]+F_{\psi}^{\prime}[\iota_{\chi}\rho]+E_{\pi}{}[\iota_{\chi}\phi]+E_{\rho}{}[\iota_{\chi}\psi]+q^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi],

(139)

where the on-shell charge,

q^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]=\mathrm{i}\int_{C}(\iota_{\chi}\psi^{A}\rho_{A}+\iota_{\chi}\phi^{A}\pi_{A}),

(140)

resembles the regular diffeomorphism corner charge up to a swap in momenta with respect to the corresponding coordinates. One can notice that

q^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]\equiv-q_{\phi}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]+q_{\psi}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]=q_{\pi}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]+q_{\rho}[\iota_{\xi}\psi],

(141)

which is due to

\displaystyle q_{\phi}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]+q_{\pi}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]=\mathrm{i}\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}(\pi_{A}\wedge\phi^{A})\overset{\xi|_{C}\in T{C}}{=}0,

(142a)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle q_{\psi}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+q_{\pi}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]=\mathrm{i}\int_{C}\iota_{\xi}(\rho_{A}\wedge\psi^{A})\overset{\xi|_{C}\in T{C}}{=}0.

(142b)

The flow equation is integrable in the same conditions as that for the regular diffeomorpshisms.

Poisson algebra

We observe that all corner charges corresponding to the gauge transformations are independent of
g
, and the only difference in the algebra of generators in the
g=0
case is the absence of the central extension in the
\{H_{\phi}[\eta],H_{\psi}[\zeta]\}
commutator.

The dual diffeomorphisms form a closed algebra with the rest of the constraints and act by Lie derivation of their parameter and swapping some of the momentum maps:

\displaystyle\{H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi],H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\xi]\}=H^{di\!f\!\!f}[[\xi,\chi]],

\displaystyle\{H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi],H^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]\}=H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[[\xi,\chi]],

(143a)

\displaystyle\{H_{\phi}[\eta],H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]\}=H_{\psi}[L_{\chi}\eta],

\displaystyle\{H_{\pi}[\lambda],H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]\}=H_{\rho}[L_{\chi}\lambda],

(143b)

\displaystyle\{H_{\psi}[\zeta],H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]\}=H_{\phi}[L_{\chi}\zeta],

\displaystyle\{H_{\rho}[\mu],H^{\overline{di\!f\!\!f}}[\chi]\}=H_{\pi}[L_{\chi}\mu].

(143c)

Let us now carry over to the study of gravity in the vanishing-coupling case.

4.2
The
g\rightarrow 0
limit of gravity

In the limit
g\rightarrow 0
, the action (
39b
) simplifies to

S^{\prime}_{SD}=\mathrm{i}\int_{M}(\pi_{A}\wedge\nabla\psi^{A}+\rho_{A}\wedge\nabla\phi^{A}).

(144)

Instead of the
\overline{\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})}
symmetry, the action is invariant under the transformation (
134
). Such a transformation is generated exactly by

H^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}]=\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}{}^{\prime}[a\psi+b\phi]+\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}{}^{\prime}[c\psi-a\phi]+q^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}],

(145)

where, similarly to the topological case, the momentum maps

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}^{\prime}[\eta]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}_{A}|_{g=0}\wedge\eta^{A}\equiv i\int_{\Sigma}\nabla\phi^{A}\wedge\eta_{A},

(146a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}^{\prime}[\zeta]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}_{A}|_{g=0}\wedge\zeta^{A}\equiv i\int_{\Sigma}\nabla\psi_{A}\wedge\zeta^{A}

(146b)

correspond to the simplified fake-flatness equations of motion, and
q^{\prime\overline{\mathfrak{sl}}}[\beta^{\prime}]
is defined in (
135c
).

Similarly, the generator of diffeomorphisms contains simplified momentum maps (
146
):

H^{\prime di\!f\!\!f}[\xi]=\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\iota_{\xi}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{\rho}{}[\iota_{\xi}\phi]-\mathcal{F}_{\phi}{}^{\prime}[\iota_{\xi}\rho]+\mathcal{F}_{\psi}{}^{\prime}[\iota_{\xi}\pi]-\mathcal{G}[\iota_{\xi}A]+q^{di\!f\!\!f}[\xi].

(147)

The results in this
Section
4
can be obtained equally well by performing an
\overline{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})}
rescaling (
136
) and setting
g=0
at any point of the calculation.

Note that the limit
g\rightarrow\infty
can be performed analogously. One starts with the action (
57a
) and performs the rescaling
\psi\rightarrow g\psi
,
\phi\rightarrow g\phi
, leaving unchanged
\rho
and
\pi
. This rescaling results in

S_{SD}\rightarrow\mathrm{i}\int_{M}(\pi_{A}\wedge\nabla\psi^{A}+\rho_{A}\wedge\nabla\phi^{A}+g^{-1}\pi_{A}\wedge\rho^{A}),

(148)

and in the limit
g\rightarrow\infty
we recover the same action in (
144
). It is quite remarkable that the different regimes,
G\to 0
and
G\to\infty
, are governed by the same action. We will explore the physical meaning of this duality elsewhere.

5
Canonical analysis of the Weyl spinor gravity formulation

In what follows, we present the Hamiltonian analysis in the continuum. Note a slight geometric notation change in this section. The starting point is to introduce a foliation, thereby assuming

M\simeq[0,1]\times\varSigma.

(149)

Thus there is a natural embedding

\varphi_{t}:\varSigma\rightarrow M,\quad\varphi_{t}(\varSigma)=:\varSigma_{t}\simeq\{t\}\times\varSigma.

(150)

In this section, we add a prefix to denote fields living on the four-dimensional manifold
M
as
{}^{4}X
and distinguish them from their pullback to
\Sigma
, denoted as
X
. We introduce a foliation by choosing a clock
t:M\rightarrow\Sigma
, which increases monotonically along every future-directed curve. Consider then a dual time-flow vector field
t\in\Gamma(TM)
such that

t^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}t=1.

(151)

Given the embedding (
150
) together with a choice of a time-flow vector field
t^{\mu}
, we can perform the
3+1
-split of the action of the self-dual gravity action (
39b
). To compute the full constraint algebra, we extend the phase space (
62
) by a connection term. The fundamental configuration variables now consist of:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{A^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{A^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{A^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{A^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}(t)

\displaystyle=[\varphi_{t}^{\ast}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathnormal{4}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.57503pt{\mathnormal{4}}\kern 6.01392pt{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-13.96501pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathnormal{4}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.57503pt{\mathnormal{4}}\kern 6.01392pt{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-13.96501pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathnormal{4}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-5.53612pt{\mathnormal{4}}\kern 3.675pt{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-9.38611pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathnormal{4}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-4.48611pt{\mathnormal{4}}\kern 2.625pt{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.3361pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}]_{a},

\displaystyle\big\{\mathchoice{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x),\mathchoice{A^{{{CD}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.97975pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.97975pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.0455pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.74678pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}}{A^{{{CD}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displayst where we introduced the following spinor-valued vector densities

with

denoting the three-dimensional Levi-Civita tensor density. All other brackets among the canonical variables vanish on the kinematical phase space.

(163d)

In addition, we also have the Gauss constraint, which obtains an extension necessary to preserve its role as a generator of an
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
gauge transformation,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}{\alpha}^{AB}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{(A}\wedge\psi_{B)}+\rho_{(A}\wedge\phi_{B)}+D\Pi_{AB}}}\right)=

(164a)

Finally, we have a constraint on the auxiliary momentum
\hat{\Pi}
, smeared with a
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
-valued Lagrange multiplier:

\displaystyle{\cal C}[\gamma]=\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\mathchoice{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\hat{\Pi}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\gamma^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\gamma^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\gamma^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}\equiv\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\Pi_{AB}\wedge\gamma^{AB}=0.

(165)

We call this constraint the
void momentum constraint
.
It ensures that the momentum
\Pi_{AB}
vanishes on-shell and, thus, the physical phase space is not affected by the extension. In particular, one can notice that the extended Gauss constraint (
164a
) differs from the smeared Gauss constraint given in (
66e
). However, on shell of the void-momentum constraint, they coincide. If, in addition, we impose all constraints simultaneously, the Hamiltonian is a mere boundary term:

H_{o}\approx H_{\partial\varSigma}=\mathrm{i}\oint_{\partial\varSigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{A}\lambda^{A}+\rho_{A}\mu^{A}}}\right).

(166)

We have recovered the result of Tung and Jacobson
45
.

5.1
Algebraic solution of the 2-curvature constraints

Before considering the constraint analysis, let us discuss here the algebraic structure of the 2-curvature constraints, which will be useful for identifying the first-class and second-class constraints of the system. In what follows, we restrict ourselves to those configurations in which the 3-forms
\psi^{A}\wedge\phi^{B}\wedge\phi^{C}
and
\phi^{A}\wedge\psi^{B}\wedge\psi^{C}
are linearly independent on
\Sigma
, i.e.
6
6

6

Upon imposing the reality conditions and restricting ourselves to a spacelike or timelike hypersurface
\varSigma
, this condition is always satisfied.

\forall a,b\in\mathbb{C}:a\psi^{A}\wedge\phi^{B}\wedge\phi^{C}+b\phi^{A}\wedge\psi^{B}\wedge\psi^{C}=0\Leftrightarrow a=b=0.

(167)

Let us expand on this condition further. First of all, we note that
\psi^{A}\wedge\phi^{B}\wedge\phi^{C}=\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon^{BC}\psi^{A}\wedge\phi_{D}\wedge\phi^{D}
and
\phi^{A}\wedge\psi^{B}\wedge\psi^{C}=\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon^{BC}\phi^{A}\wedge\psi_{D}\wedge\psi^{D}
are top-forms on
\varSigma
. Upon choosing some reference volume 3-form
d^{3}v
on
\varSigma
, we can thus find spinor fields
u^{A}
and
v^{A}
such that
7
7

7

At this point
d^{3}v
is complex, upon imposing the reality conditions,
d^{3}v
winds up to be the canonical three-volume
d^{3}v=e^{1}\wedge e^{2}\wedge e^{3}
, which is real-valued.

\displaystyle\psi^{A}\wedge\phi^{B}\wedge\phi^{C}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{2}u^{A}\epsilon^{BC}d^{3}v,

(168a)

\displaystyle\phi^{A}\wedge\psi^{B}\wedge\psi^{C}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{2}v^{A}\epsilon^{BC}d^{3}v.

(168b)

The relative normalisation between
u^{A}
and
v^{A}
can always be reabsorbed into a redefinition of
d^{3}v
. Without loss of generality, we can then also always assume

u_{A}v^{A}=1.

(169)

Notice also that the spinors
(\mathchoice{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}},\mathchoice{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$
