(173)

where
S
is a scalar,
T_{AB}=T_{BA}
is the spin-1 contribution and
\Psi_{ABCD}=\Psi_{(ABCD)}
is the spin-2 component (Weyl spinor).

Combining (
168a
) and (
168b
) with the equations of motion (
60a
) and (
60b
), it is then easy to show that all but the Weyl component vanishes,

S\approx 0,\quad T_{AB}\approx 0.

(174)

The curvature tensor can be thus written solely in terms of the spin-2 Weyl spinor:

\mathchoice{F^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{F^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{F^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{F^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}\approx\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[19.36275pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.36275pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.00803pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.57715pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{BCD}}}}{\Psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[19.36275pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[19.36275pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.00803pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}} (175)

It is important to note that this equation allows us to infer
all
components of the Weyl tensor from data solely intrinsic to
\varSigma
. The left-hand side is the curvature 2-form of the self-dual connection on
\varSigma
. The Wel spinor on right-hand side, on the other hand, is sufficient to infer all components of the Weyl tensor via

C_{A\bar{A}B\bar{B}C\bar{C}D\bar{D}}=\bar{\epsilon}_{{A^{\prime}}{B^{\prime}}}\bar{\epsilon}_{\bar{C}\bar{D}}\Psi_{ABCD}+\mathrm{cc.}.

(176)

5.2
Constraint algebra

In this section, we list the Poisson brackets among the constraints. First of all, we recall that the Gauss constraint is the generator of local
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
transformations

These equations immediately imply,

\displaystyle\big\{\mathcal{G}[\alpha],\mathcal{F}_{\phi}[\eta]\big\}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}_{\phi}[\alpha\eta],

(178a)

\displaystyle\big\{\mathcal{G}[\alpha],\mathcal{F}_{\psi}[\zeta]\big\}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}_{\psi}[\alpha\zeta],

(178b)

\displaystyle\big\{\mathcal{G}[\alpha],\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\lambda]\big\}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\alpha\lambda],

(178c)

\displaystyle\big\{\mathcal{G}[\alpha],\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu]\big\}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\alpha\mu],

(178d)

\displaystyle\big\{\mathcal{G}[\alpha],{\cal C}[\gamma]\big\}

\displaystyle=-{\cal C}[\alpha\gamma],

(178e)

\displaystyle\big\{\mathcal{G}[\alpha_{1}],\mathcal{G}[\alpha_{2}]\big\}

\displaystyle={-}\mathcal{G}\big[[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}]\big],

(178f)

where
\mathchoice{(\alpha\gamma)^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{(\alpha\gamma)^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{(\alpha\gamma)^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{(\alpha\gamma)^{{{AB}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.39691pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.63753pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.45537pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}=2\alpha^{C(A}\gamma^{B)}{}_{Ca}
.

(179f)

All other Poisson brackets among the constraints vanish. It is instructive at this point to compare our results with the situation for gravity in terms of ADM variables
3
. In the ADM formalism, all constraints are first-class, whereas there will be both first-class and second-class constraints in our formalism. This may sound slightly disadvantageous, but the case is subtle. The advantage is that the right hand side of (
179a
)–(
179e
) depends on the fundamental phase space variables at most quadratically (the curvature tensor depends on the connection quadratically). For the ADM (Arnowitt–Deser-Misner) hypersurface deformation algebra the situation is different. The ADM algebra contains structure functions that depend on the inverse metric.

5.3
Stability of constraints

In this section, we identify the conditions that are necessary for the stability of the primary constraints under the Hamiltonian evolution equations. These conditions imply no further secondary constraints. Instead, we only need to impose conditions on the Lagrange multipliers for the constraints to be preserved in time. We comment more about that in
Section
5.4
below, where we will show that the conditions on the Lagrange multipliers coincide with the time-space components of the equations of motion.

Gauss constraint

First of all, we note that the Poisson brackets (
178a
–
178f
) imply that the Hamiltonian (
160
) preserves the Gauss constraint (
164a
), i.e. for all

(182)

the constraints will be conserved, i.e. for all smearing functions
\eta^{\prime A}
and
\zeta^{\prime A}
,

\displaystyle\big\{H_{o},\mathcal{F}_{\phi}[\eta^{\prime}]\big\}

\displaystyle\approx 0,

(183a)

\displaystyle\big\{H_{o},\mathcal{F}_{\psi}[\zeta^{\prime}]\big\}

\displaystyle\approx 0.

(183b)

This is a direct consequence of the decomposition of the curvature tensor in terms of the Weyl spinor (
175
), which immediately implies

\displaystyle\Psi_{ABCD}\psi^{C}\wedge\psi^{D}=\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{ABCD}\epsilon^{CD}\psi_{E}\wedge\psi^{E}=0,

(184a)

\displaystyle\Psi_{ABCD}\phi^{C}\wedge\phi^{D}=\frac{1}{2}\Psi_{ABCD}\epsilon^{CD}\phi_{E}\wedge\phi^{E}=0.

(184b)

It is easy to check that there is no other solution for
\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}
, see
Appendix: Constraints on Lagrange multipliers
.

Next, we consider the stability of the void-momentum constraint (
165
). The Poisson brackets (
179a
–
179f
) imply

\big\{H_{o},{\cal C}[\gamma^{\prime}]\big\}\approx-\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g(\eta_{(A}\wedge\phi_{B)}-\zeta_{(A}\wedge\psi_{B)})-\lambda_{(A}D\phi_{B)}+\mu_{(A}D\psi_{B)}}}\right)\wedge\gamma^{\prime AB}.

(185)

For the constraint
{\cal C}[\gamma^{\prime}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0
to be conserved in time, the right-hand side of (
185
) must vanish for all
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
-valued 1-forms
\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{\prime A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.02249pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{\gamma^{{{\prime A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.02249pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{\gamma^{{{\prime A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.02249pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{\gamma^{{{\prime A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.31947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.02249pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.5875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}
. We thus obtain the condition

g(\eta_{(A}\wedge\phi_{B)}-\zeta_{(A}\wedge\psi_{B)})-\lambda_{(A}D\phi_{B)}+\mu_{(A}D\psi_{B)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0.

(186)

For any given configuration of
\lambda^{A}
,
\mu^{A}
and
(\psi_{Aa},\phi_{Aa})
, this is a system of
3\times 3=9
complex-valued linear equations for
\eta_{Aa}
and
\zeta_{Aa}
, which are
2\times 2\times 3=12
variables. If
(\psi_{Aa},\phi_{Aa})
are regular, i.e. the three-volume (
168a
,
168b
) is non-degenerate, these equations will be linearly independent such that the solutions to the homogenous equations

{}^{\text{\it hom}}\eta_{(A}\wedge\phi_{B)}-{}^{\text{\it hom}}\zeta_{(A}\wedge\psi_{B)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0

(187)

span a three-dimensional complex subspace of Lagrange multipliers that can be always added to any given solution to the inhomogenous equations (
186
) for
\eta_{Aa}
and
\zeta_{Aa}
. It is easy to find this subspace, generic elements can be parametrized as

where
a,b,c\in\mathbb{C}
can be naturally arranged into

(see (

In

\displaystyle\big\{H_{o},\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\lambda^{\prime A}]\big\}\approx\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{-1}D\phi_{A}\wedge\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}\lambda^{\prime B}+D\zeta_{A}\wedge D\lambda^{\prime A}}}\right)

\displaystyle\approx\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{-1}D\phi_{B}\wedge\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}-F_{AB}\wedge\zeta^{B}}}\right)\lambda^{\prime A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0.

(190)

Given the constraints and conditions on Lagrange multipliers, we can now show that this equation will be always satisfied.
Taking into account the on-shell decomposition of the curvature tensor into irreducible components, see (

), as well as the Gauss constraint
D(\psi_{(A}\wedge\phi_{B)})\approx 0
and the condition on the Lagrange multipliers
\mathchoice{\eta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\eta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\eta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\eta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}
,
\mathchoice{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}

\displaystyle F_{AB}\wedge\zeta^{B}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{F-Weyl-spinr}}{\approx}-\Psi_{ABCD}\psi^{C}\wedge\phi^{D}\wedge\zeta^{B}\approx

(191a)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{Weyl-spin-with-antisym}}{\approx}-\Psi_{ABCD}\phi^{B}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\zeta^{C}\wedge\psi^{D}-\eta^{C}\wedge\phi^{D}}}\right)\approx

(191b)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{uv-cond}}{\approx}g^{-1}\Psi_{ABCD}\phi^{B}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda^{C}D\phi^{D}-\mu^{C}D\psi^{D}}}\right)\approx

(191c)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{Gauss}}}{\approx}g^{-1}\Psi_{ABCD}D\phi^{B}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda^{C}\phi^{D}-\mu^{C}\psi^{D}}}\right)\approx

(191d)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{mu-cons}}{\approx}g^{-1}\gamma_{AB}\wedge D\phi^{B}.

(191e)

This implies that the 2-curvature constraint

is conserved on shell provided the Lagrange multipliers
\eta^{A}
,

and
\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}
satisfy (

) and (

), in other words,

\big\{H_{o},\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\lambda^{\prime A}]\big\}\approx 0.

(192)

The calculation for the second 2-curvature constraint proceeds analogously. The evolution of
\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu]=0
under the Hamiltonian flow for a spinor-valued test function
\mu^{A}
gives

\displaystyle\big\{H_{o},\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu^{\prime}]\big\}\approx-\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{-1}D\psi_{A}\wedge\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}\mu^{\prime B}+D\eta_{A}\wedge D\mu^{\prime A}}}\right)=

\displaystyle\approx-\mathrm{i}\int_{\varSigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(g^{-1}D\psi_{B}\wedge\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\gamma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}-F_{AB}\wedge\eta^{B}}}\right)\mu^{\prime A}.

(193)

Taking into account the constraints (
163a
)–(
164a
) and conditions for the Lagrange multipliers (
186
) and (
182
), we now have:

\displaystyle F_{AB}\wedge\eta^{B}

\displaystyle\approx-\Psi_{ABCD}\psi^{C}\wedge\phi^{D}\wedge\eta^{B}=

(194a)

\displaystyle\approx-\Psi_{ABCD}\psi^{C}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi^{D}\wedge\eta^{B}-\psi^{D}\wedge\zeta^{B}}}\right)=

(194b)

\displaystyle\approx-g^{-1}\Psi_{ABCD}\psi^{C}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda^{D}D\phi^{B}-\mu^{D}D\psi^{B}}}\right)=

(194c)

\displaystyle\approx-g^{-1}\Psi_{ABCD}D\psi^{C}\wedge\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda^{D}\phi^{B}-\mu^{D}\psi^{B}}}\right)=

(194d)

\displaystyle\approx g^{-1}\gamma_{AC}\wedge D\psi^{C}=0.

(194e)

This implies that there are no secondary constraints required for the stability of
\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu]=0
for all spinor-valued test functions
\mu^{A}
. In other words,

\big\{H_{o},\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu^{\prime}]\big\}\approx 0,

(195)

where
\approx
means equality up to terms constrained to vanish through (
163a
)–(
164a
) and conditions for the Lagrange multipliers (
186
) and (
182
).

In summary, there is no constraint on
\lambda^{A}
and
\mu^{A}
. The Lagrange multipliers

,
\mathchoice{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\zeta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}

and

by imposing the stability conditions for the primary constraints, see (

), (

) and (

). For teach choice of
\lambda^{A}
and
\mu^{A}
, there is a corresponding first-class constraint consisting of a linear combination of the 2-curvature, fake-flatness, and void-momentum constraints. Schematically,

\mathcal{E}^{\text{\it first-class}}_{\pi}[\lambda]+\mathcal{E}^{\text{\it first-class}}_{\rho}[\mu]\\
:=\mathcal{E}_{\pi}[\lambda]+\mathcal{E}_{\rho}[\mu]+\mathcal{F}_{\phi}\big[\eta[\lambda,\mu]\big]+\mathcal{F}_{\psi}\big[\zeta[\lambda,\mu]\big]+\mathcal{C}\big[\gamma[\lambda,\mu]\big].

(196)

The Gauss constraint (
164a
) and the 2-curvature constraints (

,
163d
) weakly commute with the primary Hamiltonian (
160
), which is a sum of constraints plus a boundary term. Thus, the Lagrange multipliers for the Gauss constraint (

) and the 2-curvature constraints (

,

) are independent gauge parameters. These correspond to
3+2+2=7
complex-valued first-class constraints. Next, we have the
3\times 3=9
complex-valued void-momentum constraints (

). The corresponding Lagrange multiplier
\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}{\gamma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.72064pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.55261pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ba}}}}
is completely determined by (

). This in turn implies that the nine void-momentum constraints (

) are all second-class. The fake-flatness constraints (

) and (

algebra, with the corresponding gauge element given in (

). The list of first-class constraints consists, therefore, of four complex-valued constraints corresponding to Lagrange multipliers

and

, three complex-valued Gauss constraints for

, see (

) and (

). The four complex degrees of freedom of
\lambda^{A}
and
\mu^{A}
correspond to the four-dimensional complexified diffeomorphism parameter given in (
73
).

In total, there are
2\times 2\times 2\times 3+2\times 3\times 3=42
complex phase-space dimensions. Counting complex-valued constraints, there are in total
2\times 2\times 3=12
fake-flatness constraints (
163a
,
163b
),
2\times 2=4
are 2-curvature constraints (
163c
,
163d
),
3\times 3=9
are void-momentum constraints (
165
), and there are three Gauss constraints (
164a
). These are 28 constraints of which there are
3+2\times 2+3=10
first-class and 18 second-class constraints. The reduced physical phase space has
42-2\times 10-18=2\times 2
dimensions, representing the two complex physical degrees of freedom of self-dual gravity.

5.4
Interpretation of the Lagrange multiplier constraints as evolution equations

In this section, we discuss how the constraints that we obtained on the Lagrange multipliers are equivalent to some of the components of the equations of motion. Going back to the definition of the Hamiltonian (
160
), and taking into account the fundamental Poisson brackets (
161a
), (
161b
), (
161c
), we determine the evolution equations for the configuration variables,

\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})

(201)

The exterior covariant derivative
D
on
\varSigma
is simply the pull-back of
\nabla
to
\varSigma
. It acts on both primed and unprimed indices, e.g.

De_{AA^{\prime}}=\mathrm{d}e_{AA^{\prime}}+\mathchoice{A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}\wedge which is precisely the condition (

).

5.5
Secondary constraint from reality conditions

So far, we have considered the Hamiltonian formulation of self-dual gravity in terms of the spinor-valued configuration variables
(\mathchoice{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}},\mathchoice{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$ , see (
161c
). The partial observables
15
,
41
for this theory are analytic functionals on a complex kinematical phase space. Hence, there are twice as many local degrees of freedom as in the real-world gravity. Let us now discuss here the reality conditions that would take us back to general relativity in terms of real-valued variables from the canonical split perspective. We recover the two real degrees of freedom of general relativity by imposing reality conditions on phase space.

Torsionless condition and reality conditions

We saw in the previous section that the purely self-dual theory has two complex degrees of freedom per point on
\Sigma
.
In what follows, we explain how the addition of the reality conditions (
214
) alters our system of constraints and reduces the number of physical degrees of freedom further down to two (real) physical degrees of freedom per point on
\Sigma
.
To achieve this, it will be enough to check how the torsionless condition is compatible with the reality conditions. This can be understood as follows.

) and the Lagrange multiplier conditions (

in which

\displaystyle\delta_{A{A^{\prime}}}:=u_{A}\bar{u}_{{A^{\prime}}}+v_{A}\bar{v}_{{A^{\prime}}}.

2\times 2
Hermitian matrices, i.e.

\delta_{B{A^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.36311pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ai}}}}{\sigma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.36311pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ai}}}}{\sigma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.36311pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ai}}}}{\sigma^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.36311pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.8308pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Ai}}}}-\delta_{A{B^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\bar{\sigma}^{{{{B^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.69069pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.69069pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.8256pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.29329pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.23048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.23048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.42502pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.29286pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{A^{\prime}}i}}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{{{{B^{\prime}}}\mathchoice{\make (210)

Reality conditions on
{}^{4}e_{A{A^{\prime}}}

We can now define the matrix-valued 1-form

e_{A{A^{\prime}}a}=\psi_{Aa}\bar{u}_{{A^{\prime}}}+\phi_{Aa}\bar{v}_{{A^{\prime}}}.

(211)

Since
u,v
are in the kernel, (
170
), this matrix
e_{A{A^{\prime}}a}
has vanishing contractions with
\delta^{A{A^{\prime}}}
. We can thus decompose it into the Pauli matrices (
209a
–
209c
):

(212)

where we set

Note that at this stage

can be complex. To guarantee that

is anti-Hermitian, we impose now the following reality conditions on the triads

\mathchoice{e^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{e^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{e^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{e^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}=(e^{i}{}_{a})^{\ast}.

(214)

In addition, we need to impose corresponding reality conditions on the time components of the tetrad, specified in terms of the Lagrange multipliers
\lambda^{A}
and
\mu^{A}
. Using the basis consisting of the (dressed) Pauli matrices (
213
) and the Hermitian metric (
208
), we can decompose the full coframe field as

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\mathnormal{4}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}e^{{\kern-6.35522pt{\mathnormal{4}}\kern (215)

where
N^{i}=(N^{1},N^{2},N^{3})
is the shift vector.
The reality conditions on the lapse and shift are then naturally

{(N}^{0})^{*}=N^{0},\quad({N}^{i})^{*}=N^{i}.

(216)

This means that the Lagrange multipliers
\lambda
and
\mu
, defined in (
155b
) and (
155c
) respectively as the time component of the spinor fields
\psi
and
\phi
, can be written as

\displaystyle\lambda^{A}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\sqrt{2}}(N^{0}-N^{3})v^{A}-\mathrm{i}\sqrt{2}N^{+}u^{A},

(217a)

\displaystyle\mu^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\sqrt{2}}(N^{0}+N^{3})u^{A}+\mathrm{i}\sqrt{2}N^{-}v^{A},

(217b)

where

N^{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(N^{1}\mp\mathrm{i}N^{2}).

(218)

The torsionless condition (
206
) can be easily split into its spatial and spacetime components.
In this way, we can infer the time derivatives of
e^{i}
and infer secondary constraints and further conditions on the Lagrange multipliers following from the stability of the primary constraints. To do so, it is useful to introduce spin rotation coefficients
\mathchoice{A^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{A^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{A^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{A^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}
relative to the spinor dyad
(u^{A},v^{A})
. These spin connections define a dressed version of the self-dual Ashtekar connection.

Spatial covariant derivatives of the spin dyad: dressed Ashtekar connection

In here, we introduce the
dressed

(221b)

where
\varepsilon_{ijk}
is the three-dimensional internal Levi-Civita tensor. The first equation informs us about the geometric role of
\mathchoice{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}
: Taking into account that the Hermitian metric

defines the internal normal to the hypersurface (recall

), we can identify the

\mathchoice{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{K^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}
on

with the
extrinsic curvature
.

Time derivative of the spin dyad: Lagrange multipliers

In the same way, we can deal with the time derivatives of the spin dyad. By its very definition, the spin dyad is a functional of the fundamental phase space fields
\mathchoice{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\psi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{\phi^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}
, see (
170
). Hence, in general we will have
\{H,u^{A}\}\neq 0
and
\{H,v^{A}\}\neq 0
. Since we deal with a normalized spin dyad, and since any two normalized spin dyads can be mapped into each other by an element of
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
, the time derivatives
\{H,u^{A}\}\neq 0
and
\{H,v^{A}\}\neq 0
determine an element of
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
. This
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
Lie algebra element can be then split into a rotation component
\varphi
and a boost component
\chi
. Furthermore, to restore covariant time derivatives, it is useful to subtract the time component of the Lorentz convection, which is given by the Lagrange multiplier
\alpha\in\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
, see (
155a
) above. We thus write

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}u^{A}

\displaystyle:=\big\{H_{o},u^{A}\big\}=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}(\varphi^{i}+\mathrm{i}\chi^{i}-\alpha^{i})u^{B},

(222a)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}v^{A}

\displaystyle:=\big\{H_{o},v^{A}\big\}=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}(\varphi^{i}+\mathrm{i}\chi^{i}-\alpha^{i})v^{B}.

(222b)

The combination
\varphi^{i}+\mathrm{i}\chi^{i}
is the analogue of the selfdual Ashtekar connection, but now in time rather than space.
In what follows, the real and imaginary parts
\varphi^{i}
and
\chi^{i}
play the role of Lagrange multipliers. This is not too surprising, given their nature as the time components of a 1-form on
M
. In a similar way,

\alpha^{i}
parametrizes the
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
-valued Lagrange multiplier
\mathchoice{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}
, see (
155a
), via

\mathchoice{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}}=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}}\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03616pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{Bi}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.21228pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebo (223)

Secondary constraints

Using all these preparations, we can now split the torsionless condition (
206
) into its time and space components. With the reality conditions given in (
214
), the spacetime components of the torsionless condition impose

\displaystyle\chi_{i}e^{i}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}N^{0}+K_{i}N^{i},

(224a)

\displaystyle\dot{e}^{i}

(227)

In summary,
by imposing reality conditions on
e^{i}
we found that the torsionless equation imposes an
an additional condition on the Lagrange multiplier
\chi^{i}
, see (
224a
), and the secondary constraint (
227
).
It is straightforward to check that the reality conditions (
214
) and the resulting secondary constraints (
227
) are second-class.
9
9

9

The probably simplest way to see this is to impose these constraints by introducing auxiliary Lagrange multipliers and adding the constraints to the action. It is then easy to see that the resulting field equations are strong enough to imply that these Lagrange multipliers are constrained to vanish. From a Hamiltonian perspective, this can only happen if the constraints are second-class.

Counting real degrees of freedom

First-class constraints, on the other hand, are in one-to-one correspondence with the undetermined Lagrange multipliers, these are given by

(230b)

), (

) and (

), (

), (

), we can see that the right-hand sides of these two equations are merely linear combinations of the Lagrange multipliers

and

and

contribute to the diffeomorphism generators. As in above, see (

), (

, see (

). The

) and (

and
N^{i}
. In summary, there are
